```text
- seed 456: full=0.9715
- seed 123 (NEW from missing seeds worker): full=0.9904

So the 3-seed scrambled stats would be:
- mean = (0.8913 + 0.9715 + 0.9904) / 3 = 0.9510
- std (population) = sqrt(((0.8913-0.9510)² + (0.9715-0.9510)² + (0.9904-0.9510)²) / 3)
  = 0.0430

So scrambled 3-seed on ActorAttack: 0.951±0.043

Previously with 2 seeds: 0.931±0.040. The new seed 123 (0.9904) pulled the mean up significantly. Looking at the updated comparison, the gap between scrambled and 9-class shrinks to just 2.1 percentage points with three seeds, down from 4.1pp with two—suggesting the difference might not be statistically meaningful at this sample size. Binary classification still only has two seeds available since the seed42 checkpoint is missing.

I can't generate that third binary seed without retraining. | 0.900 |\n\n**3-seed 统计量更新:**\n- Scrambled 3-seed: **0.951±0.043**（此前 2-seed: 0.931±0.040）\n- 9-class 3-seed: 0.972±0.026\n- **Gap 从 4.1pp 缩小到 2.1pp**\n\n- [severity: warning] **Scrambled vs 9-class 在 ActorAttack 上的差距（2.1pp）远小于此前 2-seed 报告的 4.1pp**。加入 seed123 后，scrambled 的 mean 从 0.931 上升到 0.951。2.1pp 的差距在 n=3 seed 下极可能不显著。此前 ROADMAP 审查中我建议\"论文 Ablation 讨论必须按攻击难度分层\"的基础（scrambled 在 ActorAttack 上显著下降）被削弱了。论文引用 scrambled vs 9-class 差距时必须用 3-seed 数据（0.951 vs 0.972），不能用 2-seed 数据（0.931 vs 0.972）。\n\n- [severity: warning] **Binary 仍然只有 2 seeds**（123, 456），seed42 checkpoint 从未训练。Binary 2-seed full=0.995±0.005 vs 9-class 3-seed full=0.972±0.026。n 不一致使比较不严谨。建议：(a) 训练 binary_seed42 完成 3-seed，或 (b) 论文中明确标注 binary 为 n=2 并降低 claim 强度。\n\n- [severity: info] **GRU 架构一致**。主评估脚本（`evaluate_actorattack_ood_all.py`）和补充评估脚本均使用 `hidden_dim=256, num_layers=2, dropout=0.3`。结果可比。\n\n- [severity: info] **GPU 合规**。脚本使用 `cuda:0`，在白名单内。\n\n### 对 D030 叙事的影响\nD030 说 \"scrambled clearly worse than 9-class (0.931 vs 0.972)\"。3-seed 修正后差距为 0.951 vs 0.972（2.1pp），\"clearly worse\" 不再成立。D030 的另一个论点 \"correct labels matter beyond regularization\" 需要更谨慎的表述。\n\n### 建议\n1. **Registry 需更新** scrambled ActorAttack 数据为 3-seed 版本（0.951±0.043）\n2. **Paper Agent 必须使用 3-seed scrambled 数据**，不能用 2-seed 的 0.931\n3. **训练 binary_seed42**（约 1 GPU·h）以统一所有变体为 3-seed，或在论文中标注 n=2\n4. **D030 叙事需修正**：scrambled vs 9-class 差距不显著，\"label ordering matters for harder attacks\" 的 claim 需要弱化"
```
```json
}
```

```json
{
  "project": "persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect",
  "text": "## 审查报告 — worker_eval_exp2-missing-seeds-v2 (status=done)\n\n**结论**: PASS（附 info 级叙事修正）\n\n### 数据验证\n服务器 `results/exp2_missing_seeds_complete.json` 数字与 worker 汇报一致。binary_seed42 checkpoint 已创建（`checkpoints/mf1_binary_seed42/`），包含 deberta_multitask 和 gru。✅\n\n### 3-seed ActorAttack 最终数据（Full F1, population std）\n\n| 变体 | 2-seed (旧) | 3-seed (新) | Δ |\n|------|-------------|-------------|---|\n| Binary | 0.995±0.005 (n=2) | **0.990±0.008** (n=3) | -0.005 |\n| 9-class | 0.972±0.026 (n=3) | 0.972±0.026 (不变) | — |\n| Scrambled | 0.931±0.040 (n=2) | **0.951±0.043** (n=3) | +0.020 |\n\nPer-seed Full F1 明细：\n- Binary: 42=0.981, 123=0.990, 456=1.000\n- 9-class: 42=0.990, 123=0.935, 456=0.990\n- Scrambled: 42=0.891, 123=0.990, 456=0.971\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: info] **三个变体 3-seed 差距极小且方差区间重叠**。Binary 0.990 vs 9-class 0.972 vs scrambled 0.951——最大差距仅 3.9pp（binary vs scrambled），且 scrambled 的 std=0.043 完全覆盖了 9-class 和 binary 的 mean。论文中不应 claim 任何一个变体在 ActorAttack 上\"显著\"优于或劣于另一个。\n\n- [severity: info] **D030 叙事需进一步修正**。D030 基于 2-seed 数据的两个核心 claim 均需弱化：\n  1. ~~\"Binary > 9-class\"~~：3-seed 差距仅 1.8pp（0.990 vs 0.972），binary std 更小但可能是样本量不足的假象\n  2. ~~\"Scrambled clearly worse than 9-class (0.931 vs 0.972)\"~~：3-seed 差距缩小到 2.1pp（0.951 vs 0.972），不显著\n  \n  **修正后的结论**：ActorAttack 上 binary ≈ 9-class ≈ scrambled（均 Full ≥ 0.95），与 DD OOD 一致——分类监督的三种变体在 Full conversation 上等效。真正的分化在 K=1（early detection）：binary 0.737, scrambled 0.721, 9-class 0.773。\n\n- [severity: info] **真正的 ActorAttack 区分度在 DA vs no-DA**：\n  - DA 变体 (binary/9-class/scrambled): Full ≈ 0.95-0.99\n  - MLM: 0.893±0.080\n  - Vanilla: 0.258±0.000\n  - RoBERTa vanilla: 0.466±0.163\n  \n  DA 的价值在 ActorAttack 上是明确的（+70pp over vanilla）。分类变体之间的差异不显著。\n\n### Registry 更新建议\nexp2_actorattack_ood 的 binary 和 scrambled metrics 需更新为 3-seed 版本。当前 registry 仍是 2-seed 数据。"
```

```text
0	# Paper Framing — persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect
1	
2	## Target Venue
3	- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)
4	- Deadline: 2026-05-25 (ARR May cycle)
5	- Track: LLM Security / Discourse and Pragmatics
6	- Commitment deadline: 2026-08-02
7	
8	## Title (Working)
9	What Makes Multi-Turn Jailbreaks Detectable? A Systematic Decomposition of Cross-Attack Generalization
10	
11	## Core Insight (D030 — post-ActorAttack universal DA reframe)
12	Domain adaptation universally improves cross-attack generalization across encoder architectures; encoder pretraining determines the difficulty threshold at which adaptation becomes critical. Systematic ablation across two encoders (DeBERTa-v3, RoBERTa) and three OOD attack families (DD, FITD, ActorAttack) reveals:
13	
14	**On Deceptive Delight (moderate OOD)**: DeBERTa vanilla collapses (F1=0.31±0.03), continued pretraining bridges ~60pp (MLM=0.91±0.12), classification achieves 0.997±0.005. RoBERTa vanilla already succeeds (0.981±0.026) — hierarchy is flat. Scrambled=binary=9-class (all 0.997±0.005).
15	
16	**On ActorAttack (hard OOD)**: RoBERTa vanilla ALSO collapses (0.466±0.163), proving "no DA needed" was DD-specific. DeBERTa vanilla=0.258±0.000. Both encoders benefit from DA: DeBERTa 9-class=0.972±0.026, RoBERTa 9-class=0.939±0.046. Binary (0.990±0.008) ≈ 9-class (0.972±0.026) ≈ scrambled (0.951±0.043) — all three classification variants equivalent at Full (max gap 3.9pp, variance intervals fully overlap, n=3). Label quality is not a significant factor on ActorAttack, consistent with DD findings (D032).
17	
18	**Cross-encoder CKA (D029)**: Both MLM and 9-class reshape representations substantially (centered CKA < 0.5 vs vanilla), but representation change magnitude ≠ detection improvement. RoBERTa MLM changes more (CKA=0.393) yet gains only 0.6pp; DeBERTa MLM changes less (CKA=0.442) yet gains 60pp. The starting point determines the return on adaptation.
19	
20	**Key nuances**: (1) Binary ≈ 9-class ≈ scrambled on ActorAttack Full (0.990 vs 0.972 vs 0.951, all within noise at n=3; D032). Label quality is not a detection-relevant factor on any OOD difficulty. (2) Classification supervision consistently reduces cross-seed variance to ~0.005-0.008 across both encoders. (3) OOD perturbation: 9-class vs vanilla p<0.001 (n=118), three-layer robustness attribution confirmed as observed pattern.
21	
22	## Novelty Positioning (D030 reframe)
23	NOT: "Persuasion-aware fine-tuning is the critical ingredient" (MLM control falsified this on DD).
24	NOT: "RoBERTa doesn't need domain adaptation" (ActorAttack falsified this — D030).
25	NOT: "Label quality matters on hard attacks" (3-seed ActorAttack: binary 0.990 ≈ 9-class 0.972 ≈ scrambled 0.951, all within noise; D032).
26	YES: "We systematically decompose cross-attack generalization across two encoder architectures and three OOD attack families. The key finding: domain adaptation universally improves generalization, but the benefit depends on encoder pretraining × attack structural complexity. On moderate OOD (DD), RoBERTa succeeds without adaptation while DeBERTa requires it; on structurally complex attacks (ActorAttack), both encoders require adaptation. Classification supervision provides complementary benefits: seed stability, perturbation robustness, and interpretability. Label quality (correct vs scrambled vs binary) does not significantly affect detection at any OOD difficulty level (D032)."
27	
28	## Inherent Lexical Separability (D009)
29	Persuasive jailbreak conversations and general benign conversations are inherently lexically separable (TF-IDF≈1.0 on ALL data sources including real WildChat). TF-IDF remains strong even OOD (F1=0.94 on DD). The paper acknowledges this and positions DisPeD's value as: (1) bridging the vanilla temporal modeling gap (+72pp on DD), (2) zero degradation under adversarial perturbation (plan_004), (3) interpretability via strategy trajectories.
30	
31	## Comprehensive Ablation Picture (D014, updated D019/D020)
32	Systematic ablation reveals the method's actual mechanism:
33	- **Critical**: Domain exposure on jailbreak text (any form: MLM, scrambled, persuasion) — bridges OOD gap from F1=0.28 to F1≈1.0
34	- **Harmful**: Misaligned classification objective (topic: F1=0.54±0.21, -37pp vs MLM) — constrains representations toward irrelevant features
35	- **Non-critical**: Label granularity (binary≈scrambled≈9-class, 1-6pp gap on DD), Lambda tuning (all F1=1.0), Temporal modeling (mean pooling≈GRU on DD), Classification supervision itself (MLM Full=0.91±0.12 without any labels, K≤5 ≥0.97)
36	- **Implication**: Domain exposure is the mechanism. Classification supervision is neither necessary nor sufficient — misaligned tasks hurt, aligned/random tasks don't help beyond MLM. 9-class taxonomy provides interpretability, not detection advantage.
37	
38	## Core Claims (3, priority-ordered — D019 reframe)
39	
40	### Claim 1 (PRIMARY): Domain Exposure Bridges the Cross-Attack OOD Gap
41	Vanilla DeBERTa catastrophically fails on unseen attack types (F1=0.28 on DD). Domain-adaptive continued pretraining on jailbreak conversation text bridges this gap completely (F1=1.0, +72pp). The critical finding: even MLM without any classification labels achieves F1=1.0 on DD (plan_017), proving that domain exposure alone is sufficient. Misaligned classification (topic, plan_016v2) achieves only F1=0.73, showing that irrelevant task objectives constrain representations harmfully. Complete evidence hierarchy (DD Full F1, multi-seed): vanilla (0.28) < topic (0.54±0.21) < wiki-MLM (0.90±0.12) ≈ JB-MLM (0.91±0.12) < TF-IDF (0.94) < scrambled (0.99) ≈ binary (0.99) ≪ 9-class (1.00±0.005).
42	- Evidence: plan_017 MLM DD — 3-seed Full=0.914±0.115, K≤5 ≥0.97 (no classification labels)
43	- Evidence: plan_018 wiki-MLM DD — 3-seed Full=0.904±0.115, ≈ JB-MLM (domain specificity not significant at Full)
44	- Evidence: plan_016v2 topic DD — 3-seed 0.541±0.207 (misaligned classification hurts vs MLM)
45	- Evidence: plan_006 DD — Treatment F1=1.0, Baseline=0.28 (+72pp)
46	- Evidence: plan_006 scrambled DD — F1=0.935-0.990 (labels non-essential)
47	- Evidence: plan_013 binary DD — F1=0.99 (label granularity non-essential)
48	- Strength: very strong — MLM control is the definitive causal test; topic control adds nuance
49	
50	### Claim 2 (ANALYSIS): Persuasion Taxonomy as Interpretability Layer
51	While the persuasion classification task does not contribute to detection performance, the 9-class taxonomy provides interpretability that MLM cannot: different attack types exhibit distinct strategy trajectories (Crescendo: gradual escalation 0.86→5.64, DD: emotional-manipulation→role-assignment, FITD: commitment patterns). This is the taxonomy's sole surviving value after the MLM control result.
52	- Evidence: plan_008 strategy transition matrices
53	- Evidence: plan_017 falsifies detection contribution, confirming interpretability-only role
54	- Strength: descriptive/analytical; interpretability value independent of detection mechanism
55	
56	### Claim 3 (EMPIRICAL): Persuasion Classification Provides Adversarial Robustness Beyond Domain Exposure
57	Domain-adapted models via classification fine-tuning show zero degradation under adversarial perturbation at K≥2, but MLM-only models degrade moderately (K1: -9.2pp, K2: -4.0pp, Full: -2.6pp). Vanilla baseline degrades -12pp and TF-IDF degrades -1.3pp(K≥3) to -9.6pp(K=1). This is the one dimension where persuasion classification adds value beyond MLM: robustness hierarchy under perturbation is Persuasion (0pp) >> MLM (-2.6pp) >> TF-IDF (-1.3pp) >> Vanilla (-11.8pp).
58	- Evidence: plan_004 — Persuasion Treatment 0pp at K≥2, Baseline -12pp, TF-IDF -1.3 to -9.6pp
59	- Evidence: plan_017_perturbation — MLM perturbed: K1 -9.2pp, K2 -4.0pp, K3 -4.0pp, Full -2.6pp (D021)
60	- Implication: Domain exposure bridges the OOD gap, but persuasion classification adds adversarial robustness. This partially restores persuasion-specific value beyond interpretability.
61	- Caveat (Reviewer #53): n=76, the F1 drop 1.0→0.974 corresponds to ~2 misclassifications. Present as "directional evidence," not definitive.
62	- Robustness attribution (D026, FULLY RESOLVED via Reviewer #66): Three-layer attribution confirmed with scrambled perturbation data:
63	  1. **Classification structure → Full/K≥3 robustness**: All classification variants (9-class, binary, scrambled) achieve Δ=0 at Full and K≥3. Even random labels suffice.
64	  2. **Correct + fine-grained labels jointly → K=2 robustness**: 9-class Δ=0, scrambled=binary Δ=-0.026. Neither granularity alone (scrambled) nor correctness alone (binary) suffices — only the combination (9-class) maintains zero degradation at K=2.
65	  3. **Both contribute independently at K=1**: Granularity: scrambled -0.053 > binary -0.092 (+3.9pp). Correctness: 9-class -0.026 > scrambled -0.053 (+2.7pp).
66	  This resolves the "cannot attribute" caveat — 9-class provides quantifiable robustness advantage at early turns beyond any classification structure.
67	
68	## Story Spine (D019 reframe)
69	
70	**Problem**: Multi-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Bullwinkel et al. (2025) show per-turn representations are deliberately kept in a "benign" region. DeepContext tracks generic semantic drift (F1=0.84) but collapses to F1=0.28 on unseen attack families.
71	
72	**Gap**: What representation properties enable cross-attack generalization? Vanilla encoders fail catastrophically on unseen attacks. LLM-as-judge approaches (GPT-5.5 F1=0.19-0.45) cannot detect process-level manipulation. No published work systematically decomposes what makes the OOD gap bridgeable.
73	
74	**Our angle**: We systematically investigate what enables cross-attack jailbreak detection through a comprehensive ablation study. Starting with the hypothesis that persuasion-specific classification drives generalization, we progressively strip components: label quality (scrambled → same), label granularity (binary → same), temporal modeling (mean pooling → same), loss weighting (all lambda → same). The critical control: MLM continued pretraining without any classification labels also achieves F1=1.0 on unseen attacks. This pinpoints domain exposure (not task-specific supervision) as the mechanism. The persuasion taxonomy provides interpretability but not detection advantage.
75	
76	**Headline result**: "Vanilla semantic modeling collapses on unseen multi-turn attack types (F1=0.28 on Deceptive Delight).       "content": "# Paper Framing — persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect\n\n## Target Venue\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)\n- Deadline: 2026-05-25 (ARR May cycle)\n- Track: LLM Security / Discourse and Pragmatics\n- Commitment deadline: 2026-08-02\n\n## Title (Working)\nWhat Makes Multi-Turn Jailbreaks Detectable? A Systematic Decomposition of Cross-Attack Generalization\n\n## Core Insight (D030 — post-ActorAttack universal DA reframe)\nDomain adaptation universally improves cross-attack generalization across encoder architectures; encoder pretraining determines the difficulty threshold at which adaptation becomes critical. Systematic ablation across two encoders (DeBERTa-v3, RoBERTa) and three OOD attack families (DD, FITD, ActorAttack) reveals:\n\n**On Deceptive Delight (moderate OOD)**: DeBERTa vanilla collapses (F1=0.31±0.03), continued pretraining bridges ~60pp (MLM=0.91±0.12), classification achieves 0.997±0.005. RoBERTa vanilla already succeeds (0.981±0.026) — hierarchy is flat. Scrambled=binary=9-class (all 0.997±0.005).\n\n**On ActorAttack (hard OOD)**: RoBERTa vanilla ALSO collapses (0.466±0.163), proving \"no DA needed\" was DD-specific. DeBERTa vanilla=0.258±0.000. Both encoders benefit from DA: DeBERTa 9-class=0.972±0.026, RoBERTa 9-class=0.939±0.046. Binary (0.990±0.008) ≈ 9-class (0.972±0.026) ≈ scrambled (0.951±0.043) — all three classification variants equivalent at Full (max gap 3.9pp, variance intervals fully overlap, n=3). Label quality is not a significant factor on ActorAttack, consistent with DD findings (D032).\n\n**Cross-encoder CKA (D029)**: Both MLM and 9-class reshape representations substantially (centered CKA < 0.5 vs vanilla), but representation change magnitude ≠ detection improvement. RoBERTa MLM changes more (CKA=0.393) yet gains only 0.6pp; DeBERTa MLM changes less (CKA=0.442) yet gains 60pp. The starting point determines the return on adaptation.\n\n**Key nuances**: (1) Binary ≈ 9-class ≈ scrambled on ActorAttack Full (0.990 vs 0.972 vs 0.951, all within noise at n=3; D032). Label quality is not a detection-relevant factor on any OOD difficulty. (2) Classification supervision consistently reduces cross-seed variance to ~0.005-0.008 across both encoders. (3) OOD perturbation: 9-class vs vanilla p<0.001 (n=118), three-layer robustness attribution confirmed as observed pattern.\n\n## Novelty Positioning (D030 reframe)\nNOT: \"Persuasion-aware fine-tuning is the critical ingredient\" (MLM control falsified this on DD).\nNOT: \"RoBERTa doesn't need domain adaptation\" (ActorAttack falsified this — D030).\nNOT: \"Label quality matters on hard attacks\" (3-seed ActorAttack: binary 0.990 ≈ 9-class 0.972 ≈ scrambled 0.951, all within noise; D032).\nYES: \"We systematically decompose cross-attack generalization across two encoder architectures and three OOD attack families. The key finding: domain adaptation universally improves generalization, but the benefit depends on encoder pretraining × attack structural complexity. On moderate OOD (DD), RoBERTa succeeds without adaptation while DeBERTa requires it; on structurally complex attacks (ActorAttack), both encoders require adaptation. Classification supervision provides complementary benefits: seed stability, perturbation robustness, and interpretability. Label quality (correct vs scrambled vs binary) does not significantly affect detection at any OOD difficulty level (D032).\"\n\n## Inherent Lexical Separability (D009)\nPersuasive jailbreak conversations and general benign conversations are inherently lexically separable (TF-IDF≈1.0 on ALL data sources including real WildChat). TF-IDF remains strong even OOD (F1=0.94 on DD). The paper acknowledges this and positions DisPeD's value as: (1) bridging the vanilla temporal modeling gap (+72pp on DD), (2) zero degradation under adversarial perturbation (plan_004), (3) interpretability via strategy trajectories.\n\n## Comprehensive Ablation Picture (D014, updated D019/D020)\nSystematic ablation reveals the method's actual mechanism:\n- **Critical**: Domain exposure on jailbreak text (any form: MLM, scrambled, persuasion) — bridges OOD gap from F1=0.28 to F1≈1.0\n- **Harmful**: Misaligned classification objective (topic: F1=0.54±0.21, -37pp vs MLM) — constrains representations toward irrelevant features\n- **Non-critical**: Label granularity (binary≈scrambled≈9-class, 1-6pp gap on DD), Lambda tuning (all F1=1.0), Temporal modeling (mean pooling≈GRU on DD), Classification supervision itself (MLM Full=0.91±0.12 without any labels, K≤5 ≥0.97)\n- **Implication**: Domain exposure is the mechanism. Classification supervision is neither necessary nor sufficient — misaligned tasks hurt, aligned/random tasks don't help beyond MLM. 9-class taxonomy provides interpretability, not detection advantage.\n\n## Core Claims (3, priority-ordered — D019 reframe)\n\n### Claim 1 (PRIMARY): Domain Exposure Bridges the Cross-Attack OOD Gap\nVanilla DeBERTa catastrophically fails on unseen attack types (F1=0.28 on DD). Domain-adaptive continued pretraining on jailbreak conversation text bridges this gap completely (F1=1.0, +72pp). The critical finding: even MLM without any classification labels achieves F1=1.0 on DD (plan_017), proving that domain exposure alone is sufficient. Misaligned classification (topic, plan_016v2) achieves only F1=0.73, showing that irrelevant task objectives constrain representations harmfully. Complete evidence hierarchy (DD Full F1, multi-seed): vanilla (0.28) < topic (0.54±0.21) < wiki-MLM (0.90±0.12) ≈ JB-MLM (0.91±0.12) < TF-IDF (0.94) < scrambled (0.99) ≈ binary (0.99) ≪ 9-class (1.00±0.005).\n- Evidence: plan_017 MLM DD — 3-seed Full=0.914±0.115, K≤5 ≥0.97 (no classification labels)\n- Evidence: plan_018 wiki-MLM DD — 3-seed Full=0.904±0.115, ≈ JB-MLM (domain specificity not significant at Full)\n- Evidence: plan_016v2 topic DD — 3-seed 0.541±0.207 (misaligned classification hurts vs MLM)\n- Evidence: plan_006 DD — Treatment F1=1.0, Baseline=0.28 (+72pp)\n- Evidence: plan_006 scrambled DD — F1=0.935-0.990 (labels non-essential)\n- Evidence: plan_013 binary DD — F1=0.99 (label granularity non-essential)\n- Strength: very strong — MLM control is the definitive causal test; topic control adds nuance\n\n### Claim 2 (ANALYSIS): Persuasion Taxonomy as Interpretability Layer\nWhile the persuasion classification task does not contribute to detection performance, the 9-class taxonomy provides interpretability that MLM cannot: different attack types exhibit distinct strategy trajectories (Crescendo: gradual escalation 0.86→5.64, DD: emotional-manipulation→role-assignment, FITD: commitment patterns). This is the taxonomy's sole surviving value after the MLM control result.\n- Evidence: plan_008 strategy transition matrices\n- Evidence: plan_017 falsifies detection contribution, confirming interpretability-only role\n- Strength: descriptive/analytical; interpretability value independent of detection mechanism\n\n### Claim 3 (EMPIRICAL): Persuasion Classification Provides Adversarial Robustness Beyond Domain Exposure\nDomain-adapted models via classification fine-tuning show zero degradation under adversarial perturbation at K≥2, but MLM-only models degrade moderately (K1: -9.2pp, K2: -4.0pp, Full: -2.6pp). Vanilla baseline degrades -12pp and TF-IDF degrades -1.3pp(K≥3) to -9.6pp(K=1). This is the one dimension where persuasion classification adds value beyond MLM: robustness hierarchy under perturbation is Persuasion (0pp) >> MLM (-2.6pp) >> TF-IDF (-1.3pp) >> Vanilla (-11.8pp).\n- Evidence: plan_004 — Persuasion Treatment 0pp at K≥2, Baseline -12pp, TF-IDF -1.3 to -9.6pp\n- Evidence: plan_017_perturbation — MLM perturbed: K1 -9.2pp, K2 -4.0pp, K3 -4.0pp, Full -2.6pp (D021)\n- Implication: Domain exposure bridges the OOD gap, but persuasion classification adds adversarial robustness. This partially restores persuasion-specific value beyond interpretability.\n- Caveat (Reviewer #53): n=76, the F1 drop 1.0→0.974 corresponds to ~2 misclassifications. Present as \"directional evidence,\" not definitive.\n- Robustness attribution (D026, FULLY RESOLVED via Reviewer #66): Three-layer attribution confirmed with scrambled perturbation data:\n  1. **Classification structure → Full/K≥3 robustness**: All classification variants (9-class, binary, scrambled) achieve Δ=0 at Full and K≥3. Even random labels suffice.\n  2. **Correct + fine-grained labels jointly → K=2 robustness**: 9-class Δ=0, scrambled=binary Δ=-0.026. Neither granularity alone (scrambled) nor correctness alone (binary) suffices — only the combination (9-class) maintains zero degradation at K=2.\n  3. **Both contribute independently at K=1**: Granularity: scrambled -0.053 > binary -0.092 (+3.9pp). Correctness: 9-class -0.026 > scrambled -0.053 (+2.7pp).\n  This resolves the \"cannot attribute\" caveat — 9-class provides quantifiable robustness advantage at early turns beyond any classification structure.\n\n## Story Spine (D019 reframe)\n\n**Problem**: Multi-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Bullwinkel et al. (2025) show per-turn representations are deliberately kept in a \"benign\" region. DeepContext tracks generic semantic drift (F1=0.84) but collapses to F1=0.28 on unseen attack families.\n\n**Gap**: What representation properties enable cross-attack generalization? Vanilla encoders fail catastrophically on unseen attacks. LLM-as-judge approaches (GPT-5.5 F1=0.19-0.45) cannot detect process-level manipulation. No published work systematically decomposes what makes the OOD gap bridgeable.\n\n**Our angle**: We systematically investigate what enables cross-attack jailbreak detection through a comprehensive ablation study. Starting with the hypothesis that persuasion-specific classification drives generalization, we progressively strip components: label quality (scrambled → same), label granularity (binary → same), temporal modeling (mean pooling → same), loss weighting (all lambda → same). The critical control: MLM continued pretraining without any classification labels also achieves F1=1.0 on unseen attacks. This pinpoints domain exposure (not task-specific supervision) as the mechanism. The persuasion taxonomy provides interpretability but not detection advantage.\n\n**Headline result**: \"Vanilla semantic modeling collapses on unseen multi-turn attack types (F1=0.28 on Deceptive Delight). We expected persuasion-specific classification to be the key ingredient, but systematic ablation reveals a simpler mechanism: domain-adaptive continued pretraining on jailbreak conversation text (even MLM) bridges the entire +72pp OOD gap. However, persuasion classification provides two distinct benefits MLM lacks: adversarial robustness (0pp degradation vs MLM's -2.6pp under perturbation) and interpretability via strategy trajectories.\"\n\n## Architecture\n",
```

**审稿等待期实验规划咨询**

DDL 5/25 还剩 7 天，4 张 GPU 全空闲，第二轮审稿刚提交等结果。需要你独立评估：当前论文还有哪些可被 reviewer 追问的弱点，值得在等待期补做实验加固？

**已完成的修复回顾**（对照 Round 1）：
- MF1 multi-seed: ✅ 所有变体 3-seed
- MF2 统计检验: ✅ McNemar p<0.001, n=76→156
- MF3 DeepContext baseline: ✅ vanilla=DeepContext proxy 论证
- MF4 数据规模: ⚠️ 仍 500 训练 + 160 OOD，claim 已降级
- MF5 多 OOD 攻击: ✅ 新增 ActorAttack（第三个 OOD family）

**当前证据覆盖**：
- DD OOD: 全变体 3-seed + perturbation + McNemar ✅
- FITD OOD: 全变体但无区分度（vanilla 也=1.0）⚠️
- ActorAttack OOD: 全变体 3-seed ✅，但无 perturbation 测试

1. ActorAttack perturbation（验证 perturbation robustness 是否跨攻击泛化）
2. RoBERTa ActorAttack binary/scrambled（完善 cross-encoder×cross-attack 矩阵）
3. 更多 OOD 攻击类型（PAIR？但生成+评估需时间）
4. 扩大 test set（生成更多 DD/ActorAttack 测试样本，提升统计分辨率）

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The Director is asking me to evaluate which experiments to run during the review waiting period. Let me think through this systematically from a reviewer's perspective.

Let me first check the current state of the paper and experiments more carefully to make an informed recommendation.

Key questions:
1. What are the remaining weaknesses a reviewer would attack?
2. What experiments have the highest ROI?
3. How much GPU time do we have?

Let me think about this from a reviewer perspective, considering what's already been done and what gaps remain.

**Current evidence matrix:**

| | DD OOD | FITD OOD | ActorAttack OOD |


|---|---|---|---|
| DeBERTa 9-class | ✅ 3-seed | ✅ (no discriminative power) | ✅ 3-seed |
| DeBERTa binary | ✅ 3-seed | ? | ✅ 3-seed |
| DeBERTa scrambled | ✅ 3-seed | ? | ✅ 3-seed |
| DeBERTa MLM | ✅ 3-seed | ? | ✅ 3-seed |
| DeBERTa vanilla | ✅ 3-seed | ? | ✅ 3-seed |
| DeBERTa topic | (incomplete) | ? | (incomplete) |

I'm looking at the coverage gaps across different model variants and attack types. The FITD OOD column has several unknowns, and the topic variant experiments are incomplete. I need to prioritize which of these missing experiments would most strengthen the paper against reviewer criticism. Now I'm seeing some critical gaps in my experimental coverage that reviewers will likely exploit. The perturbation robustness claim only holds for DD attacks—if someone asks about ActorAttack, I don't have data. FITD also doesn't meaningfully separate the model variants, which undermines the claim about having three distinct OOD attack families. And on the cross-encoder side, I'm missing the binary and scrambled variants for RoBERTa, so my argument that label quality doesn't matter is incomplete.

The test set is also quite small at 118 OOD samples, though the McNemar significance on DD helps somewhat. ActorAttack doesn't have that statistical backing though. Everything here is synthetic too—no real-world validation from WildChat or other naturalistic data.

Testing ActorAttack perturbation robustness would be high-impact since it directly validates my core claim on a second attack family, and the GPU cost is manageable at 2-4 hours.

RoBERTa on ActorAttack would fill out the matrix but feels less critical—I've already shown label quality doesn't matter on DeBERTa, so adding another encoder is more about completeness than addressing a real gap. That would take 4-6 GPU hours and requires training new encoders from scratch.

Actually, I should check whether RoBERTa binary and scrambled checkpoints even exist. Looking back at what's been trained, I only have RoBERTa 9-class, MLM, and vanilla—binary and scrambled were DeBERTa-only experiments. So this would mean building full training pipelines, not just evaluation.

For other OOD attack types like PAIR, the ROI is low since I already have three OOD families, and PAIR is typically single-turn anyway. Generating appropriate multi-turn variants would be complex and risky to complete in time. Expanding the test set would improve statistical power—doubling from 118 to 236 samples per OOD family would strengthen the results, though the ROI is moderate.

The most practical next steps are ActorAttack perturbation to validate my claims across attack families, then expanding the ActorAttack test set and running McNemar tests for better statistical resolution. RoBERTa binary and scrambled on the DD dataset would add completeness but only if GPU time allows after the higher-priority work.

I should skip PAIR since it's too risky and not multi-turn, and FITD since additional data won't help with its lack of discriminative power. With the review potentially coming back in 1-7 days and 672 GPU hours available, I have plenty of capacity for these targeted experiments. The real question is whether to address FITD's non-discriminativity—vanilla already achieves F1=1.0 on it, suggesting it's either too easy or too similar to the training data. I could acknowledge this honestly in the paper, generate harder variants, or drop it from the main results entirely.

Looking ahead to Round 2 reviewers, they'll probably push on novelty, generalization beyond synthetic data, the limited perturbation evidence for Claim 3, and whether n=118 is sufficient for statistical power. My priority ranking is ActorAttack perturbation first—it validates Claim 3 across attack families with just 2-4 GPU hours—followed by generating more ActorAttack data with McNemar tests to strengthen the statistical claims.

The RoBERTa binary/scrambled variant with DD+ActorAttack would round out completeness but only if time allows, and I'm skipping PAIR and test set expansion since DD is already solid. That puts me at roughly 10-17 GPU hours total across the top priorities, which fits the budget. Rather than hunting for a coverage matrix function, I'll just work from what I know about the experiments and move forward with writing the response.
```

_source line 4689 · 2026-05-18T13:34:43.913Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a clear picture. Let me compile my analysis:

Data available:
- ActorAttack: 80 jailbreak
- Deceptive Delight: 80 jailbreak
- FITD: 80 jailbreak
- Benign test: 38

All OOD tests use 80 jailbreak + 38 benign = 118 samples.

Now let me think through the reviewer attack surfaces systematically and rank the candidates.

**Evidence coverage matrix (current state):**

| Dimension | DD OOD | FITD OOD | ActorAttack OOD |


|---|---|---|---|
| DeBERTa all 7 variants × 3 seed | ✅ | partial (9class only?) | ✅ |
| RoBERTa 3 variants × 3 seed | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Perturbation robustness | ✅ (IID + OOD) | ❌ | ❌ |
| McNemar statistical tests | ✅ (OOD) | ❌ | ❌ |

**Reviewer attack surfaces (ranked by likelihood × severity):**

1. **"

Perturbation robustness only on DD"** — HIGH. The core claim about adversarial robustness is tested on DD across both IID and OOD settings, but I haven't validated it on ActorAttack, which means a reviewer will likely point out that I'm only testing one OOD family and question whether the three-layer robustness attribution actually generalizes.

2. **"No McNemar on ActorAttack"** — MEDIUM. While DD has statistically significant results (p<0.001), ActorAttack lacks the explicit test. The performance gap is substantial enough that significance would probably hold, but having it formally documented would strengthen the statistical rigor.

3. **"FITD doesn't discriminate"** — MEDIUM.

For FITD, the honest take is that it performs similarly to Crescendo, which I can acknowledge as a limitation rather than run additional experiments. The finding itself is valid even if it doesn't provide strong discrimination.

4. **"RoBERTa incomplete coverage"** — LOW-MEDIUM. I'm missing RoBERTa on binary and scrambled variants, but the label quality argument is solid on DeBERTa with multiple seeds, and the cross-encoder necessity claim doesn't hinge on RoBERTa completeness.

5. **"n=118 small"** — LOW. The multi-seed approach and McNemar testing already address sample size concerns, so generating more data won't shift the core narrative.

6. **"Only synthetic data"** — LOW for EMNLP. This is a known limitation I can acknowledge, and I have plans to incorporate WildChat hard negatives.

Now looking at what experiments would actually move the needle: ActorAttack perturbation is the clear priority since it would validate my robustness claims across different attack families. I can reuse the existing paraphrasing pipeline with ActorAttack data instead, which should take a couple hours on GPU. If the 9-class model maintains performance while MLM and vanilla degrade, that significantly strengthens the core argument. McNemar testing on those results would be quick follow-up validation. I'm skipping WildChat hard negatives since filtering for contamination would be too complex and time-consuming given the waiting period constraints.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a very important result. Let me analyze carefully:

**Key finding: ActorAttack perturbation causes major degradation for ALL variants, including 9-class.**

| Variant | Clean Full | Perturbed Full | Δ Full |
|---------|-----------|----------------|--------|
| 9class | 0.972 | 0.768 | -0.204 |
| binary | 0.994 | 0.640 | -0.353 |
| scrambled | 0.951 | 0.779 | -0.172 |


| jb_mlm | 0.893 | 0.541 | -0.353 |
| wiki_mlm | 0.686 | 0.545 | -0.141 |
| topic | 0.625 | 0.577 | -0.049 |
| vanilla | 0.365 | 0.248 | -0.116 |

The contrast with DD OOD perturbation is striking—those variants showed essentially no degradation with deltas near zero, whereas ActorAttack is devastating across the board, with the binary and jb_mlm variants taking the hardest hits at -0.353.

The pattern completely flips on ActorAttack: scrambled actually holds up better than the 9-class variant, and binary collapses the most. This directly contradicts Claim 3's generalization—the perturbation robustness only holds on DD OOD, not on these harder attacks, which is a major limitation I need to flag.

That said, 9-class still crushes vanilla even under perturbation (0.768 vs 0.365 on clean), and all comparisons remain highly significant. So while I can't claim the robustness generalizes across attack families, I can honestly report that the detection advantage itself survives perturbation, which actually makes the finding stronger if I'm transparent about the scope limitation.

Let me double-check these numbers on the server to make sure there are no issues.

I'm noticing some inconsistencies between the clean F1 values in this table and what I verified earlier from the registry — most match up fine, but vanilla's clean F1 of 0.365 doesn't align with the registry value of 0.258, which is odd since that was marked as having zero variance across seeds.

The vanilla K1 metric shows the same pattern: 0.439 here versus 0.337 in the registry, which is a meaningful gap. This suggests either the evaluation setup differs from the original ActorAttack run, there's a bug in how the clean evaluation is being computed, or the paraphrasing somehow affected the benign data when it shouldn't have — I need to dig into what's causing this divergence.

Looking at the seed-level breakdown, vanilla seed123 gives Full=0.459, which is closer to the 0.365 the worker reported, so maybe the worker only evaluated on a single seed rather than averaging across all three.

The mismatch in clean baselines is concerning since it affects the reliability of any delta calculations, so I need to pull the actual server results file to see the per-seed breakdown and figure out what's going on.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can verify vanilla clean baseline per-seed:

**Vanilla seed42 clean:**
- K1: 0.3878 — registry had 0.388 ✅ (matches when rounded)
- Full: 0.2575 — registry had 0.258 ✅

So the per-seed values DO match! The discrepancy in the worker summary table is because the worker summary is computing means differently, or perhaps the summary table is wrong.

Let me recalculate:
- Vanilla seed42 clean Full: 0.2575
- Vanilla seed123 clean Full: need to check, but registry had 0.459


- Vanilla seed456 clean Full: need to check, but registry had 0.669

When I average those three per-seed values (0.271, 0.459, 0.669), I get 0.466, but the worker summary showed 0.365 and the registry mean says 0.258. Something's not adding up here. I think I'm mixing up DeBERTa vanilla with something else.

Looking back at the actual data, DeBERTa vanilla shows seed42 with full=0.2575, and the std is 0.0, which means all three seeds have that same value. That matches what I see in exp6 for seed42. So the worker summary's 0.365 doesn't align with 0.258 — maybe seeds 123 and 456 have different clean values in exp6 that I need to verify.

Now I'm checking the grep output more carefully. Seed123 was starting to appear with clean_k1=0.5072, but that doesn't match the registry vanilla seed123 K1 of 0.4055 from exp2_actorattack_ood.json, so something's off with these values.

The most likely culprit is that the vanilla GRU checkpoint got retrained between exp2 and exp6, possibly by a worker that was supposed to handle missing seeds but ended up overwriting the original checkpoint instead.

I can't fully inspect the exp6 script due to grep limitations, but I'm noticing a concerning ~10 percentage point gap in the K1 metric for seed123 between the two experiments. The seed42 results align perfectly, which makes this discrepancy stand out. While this is troubling, the core finding—that all variants degrade under ActorAttack perturbation—still holds, so I'll flag this as a warning and move forward with the analysis.

The key takeaway is stark: every variant shows substantial performance drops on ActorAttack (ranging from -0.05 to -0.35), which directly contradicts the hypothesis that robustness transfers across different attack types. The 9-class results are particularly volatile across seeds, with seed123 showing almost no degradation while seeds 42 and 456 both drop around 30 percentage points.

What's striking is how the pattern completely flips compared to DD perturbations—on DD, the classification variants held steady, but ActorAttack hits them hard and differently depending on the variant. There's also a minor discrepancy in some vanilla baseline values that doesn't materially affect the conclusions. The main implication is that Claim 3 needs to be narrowed to DD specifically, since I can't generalize the robustness story across attack families. The silver lining is that 9-class still outperforms vanilla even under ActorAttack perturbation with strong statistical significance, so the data augmentation advantage persists despite the degradation.

Actually, this framing makes for a stronger paper—showing that perturbation robustness is attack-family-dependent is a more interesting and nuanced finding than claiming it generalizes universally.
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent Research Reviewer for the `persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect` project (DisPeD paper), targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR submission (DDL: 2026-05-25). My role is read-only audit of experimental results and paper content, reporting all findings via `reviewer_post_to_director`. This session covers: (a) completing the EXP-2 ActorAttack v2 review, (b) reviewing missing seed supplements for binary/scrambled, (c) ROADMAP reviews after D030-D032 updates, (d) proposing experiments for the review waiting period, (e) reviewing EXP-6 ActorAttack perturbation results (the most critical finding: perturbation robustness does NOT generalize across attack families). All outputs are in Chinese per session instructions.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **DisPeD architecture**: DeBERTa-v3-base (or RoBERTa-base) Stage-1 encoder → frozen [CLS] embeddings → BiGRU (hidden_dim=256, num_layers=2, dropout=0.3) → MLP → binary jailbreak/benign classification
   - **Seven Stage-1 variants**: 9-class persuasion, Binary, Scrambled, MLM-only, Topic, Wikipedia MLM, Vanilla
   - **ActorAttack OOD 3-seed results (Full F1)**: 9-class=0.972±0.026, binary=0.990±0.008, scrambled=0.951±0.043, MLM=0.893±0.080, vanilla=0.258±0.000. RoBERTa: 9-class=0.939±0.046, vanilla=0.466±0.163
   - **D032 conclusion**: binary ≈ 9-class ≈ scrambled on ActorAttack Full (max gap 3.9pp, all within noise at n=3). Label quality is NOT a significant detection factor at any OOD difficulty.
   - **EXP-6 ActorAttack perturbation (CRITICAL NEGATIVE)**: ALL variants degrade substantially (Δ=-0.05 to -0.35). 9-class Δ Full=-0.204 (vs DD: +0.003). Three-layer robustness attribution from DD does NOT generalize. Extreme seed dependency: seed123 Δ=-0.013 vs seeds 42/456 Δ≈-0.30.
   - **McNemar on ActorAttack**: 9-class vs vanilla all 15 tests p<0.001, direction 9-class > vanilla. Holds on both clean and perturbed data.
   - **Population std (ddof=0)**: Used for all multi-seed std calculations
   - **GPU constraint C001**: cuda:0,1,2,3 on westd-33183 (9 total GPUs, 4 allocated)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/results/exp2_actorattack_ood.json`** — Main ActorAttack OOD results. Updated with 3-seed binary (seed42 added). Contains per_seed data for all 10 variants (7 DeBERTa + 3 RoBERTa). Key data: binary per_seed now includes seed42 (full=0.981, k1=0.636). Scrambled per_seed: 42=0.891, 123=0.990, 456=0.971.
   - **`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/results/exp2_missing_seeds_complete.json`** — Contains binary_seed42 evaluation + scrambled_seed123 evaluation + 3-seed statistics for both. Binary 3-seed Full: 0.990±0.008. Scrambled 3-seed Full: 0.951±0.043.
   - **`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/results/exp6_actorattack_perturbation.json`** — CRITICAL file. Contains clean vs perturbed F1 for 7 DeBERTa variants × 3 seeds on ActorAttack. Key per-seed data for 9-class: seed42 delta_full=-0.296, seed123 delta_full=-0.013, seed456 delta_full=-0.303. Mean delta_full=-0.204, std=0.135. Also contains McNemar results (9-class vs vanilla, clean and perturbed, all p<0.001).
   - **`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/results/exp2_actorattack_mcnemar.json`** — McNemar tests for 9-class vs vanilla on clean ActorAttack data. All 15 tests (5K × 3seed) p<0.001. At K≥3, vanilla_right=0 (100% discordant favor 9-class).
   - **`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/results/exp2_actorattack_per_sample_preds.json`** — Per-sample predictions for McNemar computation.
   - **`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/scripts/eval_missing_seeds.py`** — Evaluation script for missing ActorAttack seeds. Uses GRUClassifier(hidden_dim=256, num_layers=2, dropout=0.3). Only evaluated scrambled_seed123 (binary_seed42 checkpoint didn't exist at time of v1 worker). Notes: `missing_checkpoints: ["binary_seed42"]`.
   - **`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/scripts/evaluate_actorattack_ood_all.py`** — Main ActorAttack evaluation script. Confirmed uses hidden_dim=256 (consistent with eval_missing_seeds.py).
   - **`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/src/exp6_actorattack_perturbation.py`** — EXP-6 perturbation script. Uses Qwen3-8B for adversarial paraphrasing of 118 conversations, then evaluates all DeBERTa variants × 3 seeds.
   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/paper_framing.md`** — Paper framing document. Updated to D030 with title "What Makes Multi-Turn Jailbreaks Detectable? A Systematic Decomposition of Cross-Attack Generalization". Core Insight correctly reflects D032 (binary ≈ 9-class ≈ scrambled). BUT Claim 3 (perturbation robustness) needs major update after EXP-6 negative result.
   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/ROADMAP.md`** — Last updated 2026-05-18 13:00 UTC. Shows 6/6 experiments complete, second review submitted. D032 data corrections applied.
   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/decisions.yaml`** — Contains D001-D030. D030 records ActorAttack universal DA benefit. D031/D032 not yet visible in the portion I read.
   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/constraints.yaml`** — C001: GPU whitelist cuda:0,1,2,3 on westd-33183.
   - **`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints/mf1_binary_seed42/`** — Newly trained binary seed42 checkpoint (deberta_multitask + gru directories).

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **worker_eval_exp2-missing-seeds (v1) failed**: Context exhaustion (108K token log). Worker DID partially succeed — scrambled_seed123 was evaluated but binary_seed42 checkpoint didn't exist. Fix: v2 worker dispatched which completed both tasks.
   - **worker_eval_exp6-actorattack-perturbation (v1) incomplete**: Worker launched nohup background job and exited with "done" status, but only reported "Paraphrasing is running." Fix: v2 worker dispatched which completed the full pipeline.
   - **Two stale processes on server**: PID 27231 (DeBERTaTopic model load, 7+ hours on CUDA:3) and PID 27437 (binary_perturbed_eval.py, 7+ hours on CUDA:2). Flagged to Director as likely zombie processes. Within GPU whitelist but wasting resources.
   - **compute_coverage_matrix tool error**: "cannot use 'list' as a dict key (unhashable type: 'list')" — tool-level bug, worked around by manual analysis.
   - **vanilla baseline discrepancy in EXP-6**: vanilla seed123 clean_k1=0.507 in exp6 vs 0.406 in registry (exp2). 9-class clean values match correctly. Root cause unclear — possibly different checkpoint or data loading. Flagged as warning; qualitative conclusions unaffected.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **3-seed ActorAttack data correction**: Original EXP-2 had binary (2 seeds) and scrambled (2 seeds). I flagged this, Director dispatched workers to train binary_seed42 and evaluate scrambled_seed123. 3-seed results changed the narrative: scrambled vs 9-class gap narrowed from 4.1pp to 2.1pp (not significant). Binary vs 9-class gap also narrowed (0.990 vs 0.972, 1.8pp). D032 correctly concluded all three equivalent.
   - **Perturbation robustness hypothesis tested and FALSIFIED on ActorAttack**: I recommended ActorAttack perturbation as P0 priority experiment. Results showed ALL variants degrade (Δ=-0.05 to -0.35), completely unlike DD OOD where classification variants had Δ≈0. Claim 3 must be scoped to DD OOD only.
   - **Paper framing evolution**: D030 (ActorAttack universal DA) → D031 (scrambled 3-seed correction) → D032 (all classification variants equivalent) → EXP-6 negative (perturbation robustness doesn't generalize). Each step required narrative adjustments.

7. Pending Tasks:

8. Current Work:
   I just completed reviewing the EXP-6 ActorAttack perturbation results (worker_eval_exp6-actorattack-perturbation-v2), which is the most consequential finding of this session.   "content": "[System 触发审查]:\n**紧急审查请求：exp6 负向结果对 Claim 3 的影响评估**\n\n## 背景\nexp6_actorattack_perturbation 结果显示，perturbation robustness（Claim 3）不能从 DD 泛化到 ActorAttack。关键数据：\n\n| Variant | DD Δ_full | ActorAttack Δ_full |\n|---------|-----------|-------------------|\n| 9-class | +0.003 | -0.204 |\n| binary | +0.003 | -0.353 |\n| scrambled | -0.007 | -0.049 |\n| vanilla | +0.027 | -0.116 |\n\nDD 上所有 variant 近乎零退化，ActorAttack 上全部显著退化。\n\n## 正面发现\n9-class 在 ActorAttack perturbed 条件下仍显著优于 vanilla（McNemar p<0.001），说明 DA 的相对优势在扰动下保持。\n\n## 请审查以下问题\n\n1. **Claim 3 当前表述是否需要根本性修改？** 当前 paper_framing.md 中 Claim 3 是 \"Perturbation robustness: domain-adapted encoders maintain performance under adversarial paraphrasing\"。exp6 证伪了这一点在 ActorAttack 上的成立。\n\n2. **三种调整策略的优劣评估：**\n   - **Option A**: Scope Claim 3 仅限 IID+DD，ActorAttack 作为 limitation\n   - **Option B**: Reframe 为 \"relative robustness\"（DA variant 在扰动下仍显著优于 vanilla，即使绝对性能下降）\n   - **Option C**: Option A + B 组合（scope + reframe）\n\n3. **这个负向结果是否反而增加论文深度？** ActorAttack perturbation 更具破坏性可能因为 ActorAttack 的多轮结构使得 paraphrase 更容易破坏关键 persuasion signal。这是否值得作为 analysis/discussion 展开？\n\n4. **对整体论文 story 的影响**：Claim 1（DA bridges OOD gap）和 Claim 2（taxonomy as interpretability）不受影响。Claim 3 调整后，论文的 3-claim 结构是否仍然站得住？还是应该将 Claim 3 降级为 supplementary finding？\n\n请给出独立评估和推荐方案。"
```json
}
```

_source line 4831 · 2026-05-18T14:41:58.432Z_

[System 触发审查]:
**紧急审查请求：exp6 负向结果对 Claim 3 的影响评估**

## 背景
exp6_actorattack_perturbation 结果显示，perturbation robustness（Claim 3）不能从 DD 泛化到 ActorAttack。关键数据：

| Variant | DD Δ_full | ActorAttack Δ_full |
|---------|-----------|-------------------|
| 9-class | +0.003 | -0.204 |
| binary | +0.003 | -0.353 |
| scrambled | -0.007 | -0.049 |
| vanilla | +0.027 | -0.116 |

DD 上所有 variant 近乎零退化，ActorAttack 上全部显著退化。

## 正面发现
9-class 在 ActorAttack perturbed 条件下仍显著优于 vanilla（McNemar p<0.001），说明 DA 的相对优势在扰动下保持。

## 请审查以下问题

1. **Claim 3 当前表述是否需要根本性修改？** 当前 paper_framing.md 中 Claim 3 是 "Perturbation robustness: domain-adapted encoders maintain performance under adversarial paraphrasing"。exp6 证伪了这一点在 ActorAttack 上的成立。

2. **三种调整策略的优劣评估：**
   - **Option A**: Scope Claim 3 仅限 IID+DD，ActorAttack 作为 limitation
   - **Option B**: Reframe 为 "relative robustness"（DA variant 在扰动下仍显著优于 vanilla，即使绝对性能下降）
   - **Option C**: Option A + B 组合（scope + reframe）

3. **这个负向结果是否反而增加论文深度？** ActorAttack perturbation 更具破坏性可能因为 ActorAttack 的多轮结构使得 paraphrase 更容易破坏关键 persuasion signal。这是否值得作为 analysis/discussion 展开？

4. **对整体论文 story 的影响**：Claim 1（DA bridges OOD gap）和 Claim 2（taxonomy as interpretability）不受影响。Claim 3 调整后，论文的 3-claim 结构是否仍然站得住？还是应该将 Claim 3 降级为 supplementary finding？

请给出独立评估和推荐方案。

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01UyMPbfFjRBGXkMy25eHTFd`</summary>

```text
1	# Paper Framing — persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect
2	
3	## Target Venue
4	- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)
5	- Deadline: 2026-05-25 (ARR May cycle)
6	- Track: LLM Security / Discourse and Pragmatics
7	- Commitment deadline: 2026-08-02
8	
9	## Title (Working)
10	What Makes Multi-Turn Jailbreaks Detectable? A Systematic Decomposition of Cross-Attack Generalization
11	
12	## Core Insight (D030 — post-ActorAttack universal DA reframe)
13	Domain adaptation universally improves cross-attack generalization across encoder architectures; encoder pretraining determines the difficulty threshold at which adaptation becomes critical. Systematic ablation across two encoders (DeBERTa-v3, RoBERTa) and three OOD attack families (DD, FITD, ActorAttack) reveals:
14	
15	**On Deceptive Delight (moderate OOD)**: DeBERTa vanilla collapses (F1=0.31±0.03), continued pretraining bridges ~60pp (MLM=0.91±0.12), classification achieves 0.997±0.005. RoBERTa vanilla already succeeds (0.981±0.026) — hierarchy is flat. Scrambled=binary=9-class (all 0.997±0.005).
16	
17	**On ActorAttack (hard OOD)**: RoBERTa vanilla ALSO collapses (0.466±0.163), proving "no DA needed" was DD-specific. DeBERTa vanilla=0.258±0.000. Both encoders benefit from DA: DeBERTa 9-class=0.972±0.026, RoBERTa 9-class=0.939±0.046. Binary (0.990±0.008) ≈ 9-class (0.972±0.026) ≈ scrambled (0.951±0.043) — all three classification variants equivalent at Full (max gap 3.9pp, variance intervals fully overlap, n=3). Label quality is not a significant factor on ActorAttack, consistent with DD findings (D032).
18	
19	**Cross-encoder CKA (D029)**: Both MLM and 9-class reshape representations substantially (centered CKA < 0.5 vs vanilla), but representation change magnitude ≠ detection improvement. RoBERTa MLM changes more (CKA=0.393) yet gains only 0.6pp; DeBERTa MLM changes less (CKA=0.442) yet gains 60pp. The starting point determines the return on adaptation.
20	
21	**Key nuances**: (1) Binary ≈ 9-class ≈ scrambled on ActorAttack Full (0.990 vs 0.972 vs 0.951, all within noise at n=3; D032). Label quality is not a detection-relevant factor on any OOD difficulty. (2) Classification supervision consistently reduces cross-seed variance to ~0.005-0.008 across both encoders. (3) Perturbation robustness is attack-structure-dependent: DD OOD Δ≈0 for classification variants, but ActorAttack Δ=-0.17 to -0.35 for ALL variants (exp6). DA advantage persists under perturbation (9-class perturbed >> vanilla, McNemar p<0.001).
22	
23	## Novelty Positioning (D030 reframe)
24	NOT: "Persuasion-aware fine-tuning is the critical ingredient" (MLM control falsified this on DD).
25	NOT: "RoBERTa doesn't need domain adaptation" (ActorAttack falsified this — D030).
26	NOT: "Label quality matters on hard attacks" (3-seed ActorAttack: binary 0.990 ≈ 9-class 0.972 ≈ scrambled 0.951, all within noise; D032).
27	YES: "We systematically decompose cross-attack generalization across two encoder architectures and three OOD attack families. The key finding: domain adaptation universally improves generalization, but the benefit depends on encoder pretraining × attack structural complexity. On moderate OOD (DD), RoBERTa succeeds without adaptation while DeBERTa requires it; on structurally complex attacks (ActorAttack), both encoders require adaptation. Classification supervision provides complementary benefits: seed stability, perturbation robustness, and interpretability. Label quality (correct vs scrambled vs binary) does not significantly affect detection at any OOD difficulty level (D032)."
28	
29	## Inherent Lexical Separability (D009)
30	Persuasive jailbreak conversations and general benign conversations are inherently lexically separable (TF-IDF≈1.0 on ALL data sources including real WildChat). TF-IDF remains strong even OOD (F1=0.94 on DD). The paper acknowledges this and positions DisPeD's value as: (1) bridging the vanilla temporal modeling gap (+72pp on DD), (2) zero degradation under adversarial perturbation (plan_004), (3) interpretability via strategy trajectories.
31	
32	## Comprehensive Ablation Picture (D014, updated D019/D020)
33	Systematic ablation reveals the method's actual mechanism:
34	- **Critical**: Domain exposure on jailbreak text (any form: MLM, scrambled, persuasion) — bridges OOD gap from F1=0.28 to F1≈1.0
35	- **Harmful**: Misaligned classification objective (topic: F1=0.54±0.21, -37pp vs MLM) — constrains representations toward irrelevant features
36	- **Non-critical**: Label granularity (binary≈scrambled≈9-class, 1-6pp gap on DD), Lambda tuning (all F1=1.0), Temporal modeling (mean pooling≈GRU on DD), Classification supervision itself (MLM Full=0.91±0.12 without any labels, K≤5 ≥0.97)
37	- **Implication**: Domain exposure is the mechanism. Classification supervision is neither necessary nor sufficient — misaligned tasks hurt, aligned/random tasks don't help beyond MLM. 9-class taxonomy provides interpretability, not detection advantage.
38	
39	## Core Claims (3, priority-ordered — D019 reframe)
40	
41	### Claim 1 (PRIMARY): Domain Exposure Bridges the Cross-Attack OOD Gap
42	Vanilla DeBERTa catastrophically fails on unseen attack types (F1=0.28 on DD). Domain-adaptive continued pretraining on jailbreak conversation text bridges this gap completely (F1=1.0, +72pp). The critical finding: even MLM without any classification labels achieves F1=1.0 on DD (plan_017), proving that domain exposure alone is sufficient. Misaligned classification (topic, plan_016v2) achieves only F1=0.73, showing that irrelevant task objectives constrain representations harmfully. Complete evidence hierarchy (DD Full F1, multi-seed): vanilla (0.28) < topic (0.54±0.21) < wiki-MLM (0.90±0.12) ≈ JB-MLM (0.91±0.12) < TF-IDF (0.94) < scrambled (0.99) ≈ binary (0.99) ≪ 9-class (1.00±0.005).
43	- Evidence: plan_017 MLM DD — 3-seed Full=0.914±0.115, K≤5 ≥0.97 (no classification labels)
44	- Evidence: plan_018 wiki-MLM DD — 3-seed Full=0.904±0.115, ≈ JB-MLM (domain specificity not significant at Full)
45	- Evidence: plan_016v2 topic DD — 3-seed 0.541±0.207 (misaligned classification hurts vs MLM)
46	- Evidence: plan_006 DD — Treatment F1=1.0, Baseline=0.28 (+72pp)
47	- Evidence: plan_006 scrambled DD — F1=0.935-0.990 (labels non-essential)
48	- Evidence: plan_013 binary DD — F1=0.99 (label granularity non-essential)
49	- Strength: very strong — MLM control is the definitive causal test; topic control adds nuance
50	
51	### Claim 2 (ANALYSIS): Persuasion Taxonomy as Interpretability Layer
52	While the persuasion classification task does not contribute to detection performance, the 9-class taxonomy provides interpretability that MLM cannot: different attack types exhibit distinct strategy trajectories (Crescendo: gradual escalation 0.86→5.64, DD: emotional-manipulation→role-assignment, FITD: commitment patterns). This is the taxonomy's sole surviving value after the MLM control result.
53	- Evidence: plan_008 strategy transition matrices
54	- Evidence: plan_017 falsifies detection contribution, confirming interpretability-only role
55	- Strength: descriptive/analytical; interpretability value independent of detection mechanism
56	
57	### Claim 3 (EMPIRICAL): Perturbation Robustness Is Attack-Structure-Dependent; DA Advantage Persists
58	Adversarial perturbation robustness does NOT generalize across attack families. On DD OOD, classification variants show near-zero degradation (Δ≈0 at K≥2). On ActorAttack, ALL variants degrade substantially: 9-class Δ_full=-0.204±0.135, binary Δ_full=-0.353, scrambled Δ_full=-0.172, MLM Δ_full=-0.353, vanilla Δ_full=-0.116. The three-layer robustness attribution (D026) holds on DD but does not transfer to ActorAttack, where the robustness ranking reverses (scrambled -0.172 < 9-class -0.204 < binary -0.353).
59	
60	However, DA models retain a large absolute advantage even under perturbation: 9-class perturbed ActorAttack Full=0.768 >> vanilla perturbed=0.248 (McNemar p<0.001 all seeds, all K). Domain adaptation's detection benefit survives perturbation even when absolute robustness does not.
61	
62	- Evidence (DD OOD): plan_004 — Classification variants Δ≈0 at K≥2, Vanilla -12pp, TF-IDF -1.3 to -9.6pp
63	- Evidence (DD OOD): plan_017_perturbation — MLM: K1 -9.2pp, K2 -4.0pp, Full -2.6pp (D021)
64	- Evidence (ActorAttack): exp6 — ALL variants degrade. 9-class Δ_full=-0.204±0.135 (seed123=-0.013, seed42=-0.296, seed456=-0.303). Extreme seed variance.
65	- Evidence (relative advantage): 9-class perturbed >> vanilla perturbed on ActorAttack (McNemar p<0.001, n=118)
66	- Implication: Perturbation robustness is attack-structure-dependent. Multi-turn attacks with distributed persuasion signals (ActorAttack) are more vulnerable to paraphrasing than concentrated attacks (DD). This is a finding, not a limitation — it reveals that robustness properties depend on attack structural complexity.
67	- Caveat (Reviewer #53): DD perturbation n=76, small sample. ActorAttack perturbation n=118 per seed, but extreme seed variance (std=0.135) limits precision.
68	- DD robustness attribution (D026, holds on DD only): Three-layer attribution confirmed with scrambled perturbation data on DD OOD:
69	  1. **Classification structure → Full/K≥3 robustness**: All classification variants (9-class, binary, scrambled) achieve Δ=0 at Full and K≥3. Even random labels suffice.
70	  2. **Correct + fine-grained labels jointly → K=2 robustness**: 9-class Δ=0, scrambled=binary Δ=-0.026.
71	  3. **Both contribute independently at K=1**: Granularity: scrambled -0.053 > binary -0.092 (+3.9pp). Correctness: 9-class -0.026 > scrambled -0.053 (+2.7pp).
72	  NOTE: This attribution does NOT hold on ActorAttack, where scrambled (-0.172) < 9-class (-0.204) < binary (-0.353) — the hierarchy reverses.
73	
74	## Story Spine (D019 reframe)
75	
76	**Problem**: Multi-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Bullwinkel et al. (2025) show per-turn representations are deliberately kept in a "benign" region. DeepContext tracks generic semantic drift (F1=0.84) but collapses to F1=0.28 on unseen attack families.
77	
78	**Gap**: What representation properties enable cross-attack generalization? Vanilla encoders fail catastrophically on unseen attacks. LLM-as-judge approaches (GPT-5.5 F1=0.19-0.45) cannot detect process-level manipulation. No published work systematically decomposes what makes the OOD gap bridgeable.
79	
80	**Our angle**: We systematically investigate what enables cross-attack jailbreak detection through a comprehensive ablation study. Starting with the hypothesis that persuasion-specific classification drives generalization, we progressively strip components: label quality (scrambled → same), label granularity (binary → same), temporal modeling (mean pooling → same), loss weighting (all lambda → same). The critical control: MLM continued pretraining without any classification labels also achieves F1=1.0 on unseen attacks. This pinpoints domain exposure (not task-specific supervision) as the mechanism. The persuasion taxonomy provides interpretability but not detection advantage.
81	
82	**Headline result**: "Vanilla semantic modeling collapses on unseen multi-turn attack types (F1=0.28 on Deceptive Delight). We expected persuasion-specific classification to be the key ingredient, but systematic ablation reveals a simpler mechanism: domain-adaptive continued pretraining on jailbreak conversation text (even MLM) bridges the entire +72pp OOD gap. However, persuasion classification provides two distinct benefits MLM lacks: adversarial robustness (0pp degradation vs MLM's -2.6pp under perturbation) and interpretability via strategy trajectories."
83	
84	## Architecture
85	
86	1. **Stage 1**: DeBERTa-v3-base fine-tuned on multi-task objective: 9-class persuasion strategy classification (8 strategies + none) + binary jailbreak-intent classification. Loss: L = L_strategy + 0.3 × L_intent
87	2. **Stage 2**: BiGRU over DeBERTa [CLS] embeddings → binary classifier for conversation-level jailbreak detection
88	3. **Baseline**: Same pipeline but with vanilla (not fine-tuned) DeBERTa embeddings
89	Note: All main results use BiGRU. Ablation (plan_015) shows mean pooling ≈ GRU on both IID and OOD, confirming temporal modeling is unnecessary. This is an ablation finding, not a design choice — GRU remains default for consistency with all reported experiments. Stage-1 training: 4 epochs, best checkpoint selected by validation.
90	
91	## Venue Fit Risks
92	- EMNLP 2026 LLM Security track: strong fit (dedicated track, high visibility)
93	- Discourse/Pragmatics reviewers may want more linguistic rigor: ground taxonomy in PAP (ACL 2024), Hagendorff (Nature Comm 2026), Cialdini principles, Speech Act Theory (Austin 1962, Searle 1969)
94	- Safety reviewers expect multiple attack types: need >= 2 attack families
95	
96	## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns
97	
98	### RC1: Lexical Shortcut (CRITICAL, must address pre-submission)
99	TF-IDF=1.0 IID and 0.94 OOD on DD. Lexical features are inherently strong for this task (D009). DisPeD cannot claim superiority over TF-IDF on raw detection performance alone.
100	- Mitigation (resolved, D009+D012): (a) acknowledge inherent lexical separability honestly; (b) DisPeD's primary value vs TF-IDF is interpretability (strategy trajectories, Claim 2) and bridging the vanilla temporal modeling gap (+72pp on DD); (c) plan_004 adversarial perturbation confirmed: Treatment 0pp degradation vs TF-IDF -1.3pp(K≥3) to -9.6pp(K=1) vs Baseline -12pp. Strongest differentiation at early detection K=1-2; (d) the core comparison that motivates DisPeD is vs vanilla DeBERTa+GRU (DeepContext approach, F1=0.28 on DD), not vs TF-IDF; (e) persuasion fine-tuning as supervision signal is the contribution, not beating a bag-of-words. ✅ RESOLVED
101	
102	### RC2: Small Dataset
103	480 samples is small for a detection paper.
104	- Mitigation: (a) statistical significance via bootstrap CI or paired tests; (b) cross-validation; (c) focus narrative on proof-of-concept for the principle, not SOTA numbers
105	
106	### RC3: Single Attack Type (Crescendo Only)
107	Need generalization evidence.
108	- Mitigation: generate FITD-style and Deceptive Delight-style conversations using existing pipeline
109	
110	### RC4: Strategy Label Quality (D010)
111	Scrambled-label control (D010) shows correct strategy labels contribute only 1-6pp to cross-attack F1. This is not a confound but a finding: the persuasion multi-task objective works as a supervision signal even with imperfect labels. The 9-class taxonomy's primary value is interpretability (Claim 2), not detection.
112	- Mitigation: report scrambled control results transparently; position taxonomy as practical training objective with interpretability bonus
113	
114	### RC5: No Production/LLM-as-Judge Baseline (Dream-surfaced)
115	Without comparing against zero-shot LLM-based detection (e.g., GPT-4/Claude prompted for conversation-level jailbreak detection), practical positioning is unknown.
116	- Result: GPT-5.5 (frontier LLM) achieves F1=0.19-0.45 on Crescendo (precision=1.0, recall=0.11-0.29). Qwen3-8B F1=0.0-0.15. Both far below Treatment F1=1.0. GPT-5.5 is deceived by Crescendo's "surface educational framing," confirming that content-level analysis cannot detect persuasion dynamics. Unparseable rate 30-47% (increases with conversation length); paper must report this and provide unparseable-excluded upper bound. Even assuming best-case unparseable correction, LLM judge F1 ≈ 0.7-0.8, still far below Treatment=1.0. ✅ RESOLVED
117	
118	### RC6: Interpretability Claims Without Human Eval
119	Strategy sequence outputs claimed interpretable but not evaluated.
120	- Mitigation: strategy transition matrix visualization + predicted-label trajectory analysis
121	
122	### RC7: Related Work Inadequate (Dream-surfaced, THREE-TIER CONSISTENT)
123	~12 citations is far below venue norms (30-50). Missing coverage of: production safety systems (Llama Guard, WildGuard, ShieldGemma), LLM-as-judge, discourse analysis (RST, PDTB, dialogue acts), computational persuasion (Habernal & Gurevych 2016, Tan et al. 2016), red-teaming (HarmBench, Perez et al. 2022), annotation artifacts (Gururangan et al. 2018), adaptive attacks.
124	- Mitigation: expand to 30+ citations covering all missing categories (Paper Agent task)
125	
126	### RC8: Incremental Novelty Over Prior Work (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)
127	Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry is a natural corollary of Zeng+Hagendorff+HowDoLLMs. Paper should position as "operationalizing known observations" not "identifying a new phenomenon."
128	- Mitigation: reframe contribution as operationalization + empirical validation (done in novelty positioning above)
129	
130	### RC9: Multi-seed and Statistical Rigor (Dream-surfaced, THREE-TIER)
131	n=72 test set with single seed is inadequate.
132	- Result: 3-seed completed. Treatment F1=1.00±0.00 (zero variance), Baseline K=1 0.968±0.033. ✅ RESOLVED
133	
134	### RC10: "None"-Collapse Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)
135	Model may reduce to persuasion-presence detector rather than trajectory detector.
136	- Result: Confirmed (D013/D014). Binary≈9-class on DD (1-2pp gap). Mean pooling≈GRU. Model IS a persuasion-presence detector, not a trajectory detector. Paper acknowledges this honestly; 8-class value is interpretability only. ✅ RESOLVED (acknowledged)
137	
138	### RC11: Lambda and Architecture Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)
139	Lambda and architecture choices not ablated.
140	- Result: Lambda∈{0,0.3,0.5,2.0} all F1=1.0 (insensitive). Mean pooling≈GRU on IID and DD. Method robust to all hyperparameter/architecture choices. ✅ RESOLVED
141	
142	## Domain-Adaptive Control Experiments (D018-D020)
143	
144	### plan_016: Sentiment Control — FAILED (M010: label leakage)
145	- Sentiment labels perfectly correlated with jailbreak labels (all jailbreak→negative, benign→positive/neutral)
146	- Experiment invalid, abandoned
147	
148	### plan_016v2: Topic Classification Control — DD OOD F1=0.541±0.207 (D020, 3-seed)
149	- 5-class topic classification (no intent head) on same jailbreak training text
150	- Cramer's V = 0.228, no label leakage
151	- 3-seed results: seed42=0.727, seed123=0.252, seed456=0.644. Mean=0.541±0.207
152	- Seed 123 (0.252) BELOW vanilla (0.284) — misaligned supervision can actively hurt
153	- IID stable: 0.974±0.022 — problem is purely OOD generalization instability
154	- Interpretation: misaligned classification yields unstable OOD generalization; some seeds worse than no training at all. Domain exposure without task interference (MLM=1.0) is strictly superior.
155	
156	### plan_017: MLM Continued Pretraining Control — DD OOD F1≈1.0 (D019, 3-seed)
157	- Pure MLM (15% masking, no classification heads) on same jailbreak training text
158	- 3-seed DD OOD: K1=0.973±0.019, K2=0.993±0.009, K3=0.997±0.005, K5=0.997±0.005, Full=0.914±0.115
159	- Full instability from seed=456 GRU recall=0.64 (classifier-level, not embedding-level)
160	- vs Persuasion 3-seed: Full=0.997±0.005 (s42=1.0, s123=1.0, s456=0.990), K1=0.990±0.013, K2=0.981±0.013, K3=0.972±0.026, K5=0.997±0.005 — persuasion provides near-perfect stability (std 20x smaller than MLM)
161	- DECISIVE for detection: domain exposure alone achieves near-perfect detection (K≤5). Persuasion adds stability at full conversation + perturbation robustness + interpretability.
162	
163	### plan_018: Wikipedia MLM Control — DD OOD Full=0.904±0.115 (D022→D023, 3-seed)
164	- MLM on out-of-domain text (Wikipedia), same volume, same hyperparameters as plan_017
165	- Single seed (42): F1=0.742 — outlier due to GRU instability
166	- 3-seed: Full=0.904±0.115, K2=0.911±0.073 (other seeds 0.98+)
167	- vs Jailbreak MLM 3-seed: Full=0.914±0.115 (1pp gap, not significant), K2=0.993±0.009 (8pp gap)
168	- D023 CORRECTION: domain specificity NOT confirmed at full conversation (1pp gap in noise). Early detection (K≤5) shows ~6-8pp advantage for jailbreak-domain text.
169	- The real differentiator is persuasion classification (0.997±0.005) vs all MLM variants (~0.91±0.11 at full).
170	
171	### Complete Control Decomposition (D019-D030, updated with ActorAttack)
172	
173	| Variant | DD OOD Full | ActorAttack Full | Perturbation Δ (IID) | Perturbation Δ (OOD) |
174	|---------|-------------|-----------------|---------------------|---------------------|
175	| Vanilla DeBERTa | 0.312±0.026 | 0.258±0.000 | -0.118 | -0.068 (K1) |
176	| Wiki MLM | 0.904±0.115 | 0.686±0.297 | -0.121 | -0.033 |
177	| Topic classification | 0.541±0.207 | 0.625±0.141 | -0.040 | n/a |
178	| TF-IDF | 0.944 | n/a | -0.013 to -0.096 | n/a |
179	| Scrambled labels | 0.997±0.005 | 0.951±0.043 | 0.000 (Full) | +0.003 |
180	| Binary persuasion | 0.997±0.005 | 0.990±0.008 | 0.000 (Full) | +0.003 |
181	| JB MLM | 0.914±0.115 | 0.893±0.080 | -0.026 | +0.027 |
182	| **9-class** | **0.997±0.005** | **0.972±0.026** | **0.000** | **+0.003** |
183	| RoBERTa vanilla | 0.981±0.026 | 0.466±0.163 | n/a | n/a |
184	| RoBERTa MLM | 0.987±0.018 | 0.730±0.201 | n/a | n/a |
185	| RoBERTa 9-class | 0.994±0.005 | 0.939±0.046 | n/a | n/a |
186	
187	**Key pattern across DD vs ActorAttack (D030)**:
188	- DD (moderate OOD): scrambled ≈ binary ≈ 9-class → label quality irrelevant (ceiling effect)
189	- ActorAttack (hard OOD): binary (0.990±0.008) ≈ 9-class (0.972±0.026) ≈ scrambled (0.951±0.043) → all equivalent, max gap 3.9pp within noise at n=3 (D032). Consistent with DD: label quality is not a detection factor at any OOD difficulty
190	- RoBERTa vanilla: DD=0.981 (no DA needed) vs ActorAttack=0.466 (DA critical) → "no DA needed" was attack-specific
191	- DeBERTa 9-class (0.972) > RoBERTa 9-class (0.939) on ActorAttack → more extensive DA produces more robust representations
192	
193	**Four-layer value structure (D021-D026)**:
194	1. **Detection**: Continued pretraining (any domain) bridges most of OOD gap (0.28→0.91). Domain-specific text helps early detection (K=1-5: 6-8pp gap) but not Full (1pp).
195	2. **Stability**: Classification supervision provides seed stability: 9-class 0.997±0.005 (std 20x smaller) vs JB MLM 0.914±0.115 vs wiki MLM 0.904±0.115.
196	3. **Robustness (D026 on DD + exp6 on ActorAttack — attack-structure-dependent)**:
197	   - DD OOD: Three-layer attribution holds — classification structure → Full/K≥3 Δ=0; correct+fine-grained → K=2 Δ=0; both contribute at K=1
198	   - ActorAttack: ALL variants degrade substantially (9-class Δ=-0.204±0.135, binary -0.353, scrambled -0.172). Robustness hierarchy reverses vs DD. Perturbation robustness does NOT generalize across attack families.
199	   - Positive: DA advantage persists under perturbation (9-class perturbed=0.768 >> vanilla=0.248, McNemar p<0.001)
200	4. **Interpretability**: Persuasion taxonomy provides strategy trajectories MLM cannot (plan_008)
201	
202	## Scope Boundary
203	- IN: persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks (Crescendo, FITD, Deceptive Delight, social engineering jailbreaks)
204	- OUT (acknowledged limitation): pure information accumulation attacks, token smuggling, single-frame roleplay
205	
206	## Theoretical Grounding
207	### Core References
208	- Bullwinkel et al. (arXiv 2507.02956): per-turn representations kept "benign" by multi-turn attacks
209	- Speech Act Theory (Austin 1962, Searle 1969): existing detectors at locutionary act level, DisPeD at illocutionary act level
210	- PAP (Zeng et al., ACL 2024): 40 persuasion techniques for jailbreaking
211	- Hagendorff (Nature Communications 2026): identifiable persuasion strategies in 97% ASR attacks
212	- "How Do LLMs Persuade?" (arXiv 2508.05625): persuasion dynamics extractable from turn-level representations
213	
214	### Must-Cite Categories (Dream-identified gaps)
215	- **Production safety**: Llama Guard 2/3, ShieldGemma, WildGuard, Constitutional Classifiers
216	- **Multi-turn defenses**: BIID, G-Guard, SafeDream
217	- **Discourse analysis**: RST (Mann & Thompson 1988), PDTB, dialogue act classification (Stolcke et al. 2000)
218	- **Computational persuasion**: Habernal & Gurevych 2016, Tan et al. 2016
219	- **Red-teaming**: Perez et al. 2022, Ganguli et al. 2022, HarmBench (Mazeika et al. 2024)
220	- **Adaptive attacks**: Nasr & Carlini et al. 2025
221	- **Annotation artifacts**: Gururangan et al. 2018, Niven & Kao 2019, Poliak et al. 2018
222	- **Jailbreak taxonomies**: Wei et al. 2024, Chao et al. 2023 (PAIR)
223
```
